Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um, I hope I'm not overreacting or wasting your time—I know you must be very busy. 

I guess I came in because... well, I just can't seem to shut my brain off anymore. I'm sure a lot of people deal with work stress, right? But for me, it's like it starts with just one small concern. Like, say a client isn't responding to an email. Pretty normal stuff. But within minutes, I'm somehow completely convinced I'm going to lose the entire contract. And then... well, I'm a little embarrassed to even admit this, but my mind just spirals. I start panicking that I'm going to fall behind on my divorce payments, and that I'll just never get back to a real leadership role again.

I start panicking that I'm going to fall behind on my divorce payments, and that I'll just never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nIt just sort of runs away from me. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay. I hope it's okay to share this, but it really started, um, getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's when I... well, I was let go from my previous job. I know, it's not a great look, but they were downsizing and I was just one of the ones they let go. \n\nAnyway, that's when the spiraling really kicked in. And, you know, I thought it would get better once I got back on my feet. But even after I found some contract work, it just... it never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panic mode and forgot how to turn off. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get to me this much since I do have work now. Does that timeline sound helpful to you?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I hope I'm explaining it okay. Is that the kind of thing you help people with?\n\nDoctor: Did these racing thoughts and panicking start after something significant happened in your life, like your divorce?\n\nPatient: Well, the divorce was definitely stressful, but... no, I wouldn't say it started then. I hope it's okay to share this, but it really started, um, getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's when I... well, I was let go from my previous job. I know, it's not a great look, but they were downsizing and I was just one of the ones they let go. \n\nAnyway, that's when the spiraling really kicked in. And, you know, I thought it would get better once I got back on my feet. But even after I found some contract work, it just... it never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panic mode and forgot how to turn off. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get to me this much since I do have work now. Does that timeline sound helpful to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time since you started experiencing these racing thoughts and panic?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad most of the time. I mean, sure, everyone gets a little down now and then, especially with the job transition and everything, but I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything like that. I really try to stay positive and keep things in perspective. It's really just the worrying that's the issue, you know? The feeling that the other shoe is always about to drop. But I don't sit around feeling sad all day, I promise. I'm actually pretty upbeat most of the time. Is it okay that I don't feel sad? I hope that doesn't mean I'm in the wrong place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life, I promise. I know how important it is to just push through. But... well, sometimes it's just so overwhelming. \n\nLike, with work, I've... I've sort of missed a few networking follow-ups recently. And, um, I might have delayed a couple of client deliverables. Not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong with them, and then I just freeze. I know that's not a good excuse, and I'm really trying to do better. \n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I used to play in a weekend soccer league, and hang out with friends a lot. But lately, I've kind of pulled back from all that. I just... I get so tense and irritable with all the worrying, and honestly, I'm just really embarrassed for people to see me like that. I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. 

But I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.

But I don't sit around feeling sad all day, I promise. I'm actually pretty upbeat most of the time. Is it okay that I don't feel sad? I hope that doesn't mean I'm in the wrong place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life, I promise. I know how important it is to just push through. But... well, sometimes it's just so overwhelming. \n\nLike, with work, I've... I've sort of missed a few networking follow-ups recently. And, um, I might have delayed a couple of client deliverables. Not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong with them, and then I just freeze. I know that's not a good excuse, and I'm really trying to do better. \n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I used to play in a weekend soccer league, and hang out with friends a lot. But lately, I've kind of pulled back from all that. I just... I get so tense and irritable with all the worrying, and honestly, I'm just really embarrassed for people to see me like that. I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. That's... that's probably just a normal reaction to how competitive the IT field is right now, though, right? I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely irrational.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your health or the health of loved ones?\n\nPatient: Health? Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Like, doing the exact same thing over and over? No, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, I have my daily routines to keep my schedule on track, but I don't feel forced to repeat things or anything like that. I try really hard to just do a task efficiently and move on, you know, like a good manager should. I know some people have to, like, check locks a dozen times or wash their hands over and over, but I promise I don't have any habits like that. I really try to keep my behaviors as normal as possible. \n\nThat's... that's a good sign, right? That I don't have anything like that going on? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Social situations? Oh, um... no, no, I don't avoid them.

Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But nothing too crazy, I promise. I try really hard to keep everything running smoothly.

But lately, I've kind of pulled back from all that. I just... I get so tense and irritable with all the worrying, and honestly, I'm just really embarrassed for people to see me like that. I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. Just because I want them to be perfect, you know? I get a little stuck trying to anticipate every single thing that could possibly go wrong with the systems, so I end up taking a bit longer. And I guess I've missed a couple of networking follow-ups, too. It's just... I want to make sure I say exactly the right thing so I don't look incompetent. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... no. No, I don't have anything like that. 

I mean, obviously I think about my job a lot, and I'll go over my checklists for the IT department in my head, but it's not like a broken record that I can't turn off. It's just... normal preparation, you know? I definitely don't have weird or bad thoughts getting stuck in my head against my will. That's... that's how it's supposed to be, right? Being able to manage what you're thinking about? I just want to make sure I'm being clear and giving you the right answers.

But within minutes, I'm somehow completely convinced I'm going to lose the entire contract. And then... well, I'm a little embarrassed to even admit this, but my mind just spirals. I start panicking that I'm going to fall behind on my divorce payments, and that I'll just never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nIt just sort of runs away from me. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay. Is that the kind of thing you help people with?\n\nDoctor: Did these racing thoughts and panicking start after something significant happened in your life, like your divorce?\n\nPatient: Well, the divorce was definitely stressful, but... no, I wouldn't say it started then. I hope it's okay to share this, but it really started, um, getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's when I... well, I was let go from my previous job. I know, it's not a great look, but they were downsizing and I was just one of the ones they let go. \n\nAnyway, that's when the spiraling really kicked in. And, you know, I thought it would get better once I got back on my feet. But even after I found some contract work, it just... it never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panic mode and forgot how to turn off. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't let it get to me this much since I do have work now. Does that timeline sound helpful to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time since you started experiencing these racing thoughts and panic?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad most of the time. I mean, sure, everyone gets a little down now and then, especially with the job transition and everything, but I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything like that. I really try to stay positive and keep things in perspective. It's really just the worrying that's the issue, you know? The feeling that the other shoe is always about to drop. But I don't sit around feeling sad all day, I promise. I'm actually pretty upbeat most of the time. Is it okay that I don't feel sad? I hope that doesn't mean I'm in the wrong place.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life, I promise. I know how important it is to just push through. But... well, sometimes it's just so overwhelming. \n\nLike, with work, I've... I've sort of missed a few networking follow-ups recently. And, um, I might have delayed a couple of client deliverables. Not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong with them, and then I just freeze. I know that's not a good excuse, and I'm really trying to do better. \n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I used to play in a weekend soccer league, and hang out with friends a lot. But lately, I've kind of pulled back from all that. I just... I get so tense and irritable with all the worrying, and honestly, I'm just really embarrassed for people to see me like that. I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. That's... that's probably just a normal reaction to how competitive the IT field is right now, though, right? I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely irrational.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your health or the health of loved ones?\n\nPatient: Health? Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Like, doing the exact same thing over and over? No, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, I have my daily routines to keep my schedule on track, but I don't feel forced to repeat things or anything like that. I try really hard to just do a task efficiently and move on, you know, like a good manager should. I know some people have to, like, check locks a dozen times or wash their hands over and over, but I promise I don't have any habits like that. I really try to keep my behaviors as normal as possible. \n\nThat's... that's a good sign, right? That I don't have anything like that going on? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any social situations that you avoid because they make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Social situations? I really try not to let it affect my day-to-day life, I promise. I know how important it is to just push through. But... well, sometimes it's just so overwhelming. \n\nLike, with work, I've... I've sort of missed a few networking follow-ups recently. And, um, I might have delayed a couple of client deliverables. Not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong with them, and then I just freeze. I know that's not a good excuse, and I'm really trying to do better. \n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I used to play in a weekend soccer league, and hang out with friends a lot. But lately, I've kind of pulled back from all that. I just... I get so tense and irritable with all the worrying, and honestly, I'm just really embarrassed for people to see me like that. I don't want to bring anyone else down, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. That's... that's probably just a normal reaction to how competitive the IT field is right now, though, right? I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely irrational.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your health or the health of loved ones?\n\nPatient: Health? Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Like, doing the exact same thing over and over? No, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, I have my daily routines to keep my schedule on track, but I don't feel forced to repeat things or anything like that. I try really hard to just do a task efficiently and move on, you know, like a good manager should. I know some people have to, like, check locks a dozen times or wash their hands over and over, but I promise I don't have any habits like that. I really try to keep my behaviors as normal as possible. \n\nThat's... that's a good sign, right? That I don't have anything like that going on? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any social situations that you avoid because they make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Social situations? Oh, um... no, no, I don't avoid them. \n\nI mean, as an IT manager, I'm in meetings all day, so I really can't just hide away, you know? I always make sure I show up and do my part. I might get just a little bit... well, just the normal amount of nervous before a big presentation to the executives or something, but I never skip them. I push right through because I want to be reliable for my team. \n\nAnd I don't shy away from office get-togethers or dinners or anything like that, either. I really try to be friendly and get along with everyone. I don't want anyone to think I'm antisocial or not a team player. \n\nThat's... that's a good thing, right? That I'm able to handle being around people without avoiding it? I just want to make sure I'm functioning the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or tasks that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But nothing too crazy, I promise. I try really hard to keep everything running smoothly. \n\nI mean, work-wise, I'm still getting things done, but I might have... delayed a few client deliverables lately. Just because I want them to be perfect, you know? I get a little stuck trying to anticipate every single thing that could possibly go wrong with the systems, so I end up taking a bit longer. And I guess I've missed a couple of networking follow-ups, too. It's just... I want to make sure I say exactly the right thing so I don't look incompetent. That's a pretty normal way for a manager to operate, right? Being thorough?\n\nAnd outside of work... well, I used to play in a weekend soccer league. I haven't been going as much recently. Or seeing my friends as often. It's not that I don't want to! It's just... I've been feeling so tense and, honestly, a little irritable lately, and I'm kind of embarrassed about it. I don't want to snap at anyone or ruin their good time, so it just seems better to step back a little until I can get myself sorted out. \n\nI really am trying my best not to let it affect anyone else, though. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keeping it to myself so I'm not a burden?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no. No, I don't have anything like that. \n\nI mean, obviously I think about my job a lot, and I'll go over my checklists for the IT department in my head, but it's not like a broken record that I can't turn off. It's just... normal preparation, you know? I definitely don't have weird or bad thoughts getting stuck in my head against my will. I try really hard to keep my mind focused and productive, like a professional should. \n\nIf I need to focus on something else, I... I can. I'm in control of my thoughts, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm losing my grip or anything like that. That's... that's how it's supposed to be, right? Being able to manage what you're thinking about? I just want to make sure I'm being clear and giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain mental or physical actions in response to your worries?\n\nPatient: Actions? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say I worry about *everything*. I just like to stay on top of my responsibilities, you know? I think anyone in my position would. 

But... I guess my mind does sort of jump around a bit. Like, I'll be thinking about my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments, and then... well, to be honest, I sometimes worry about my professional reputation. I'll catch myself wondering if people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm... you know, damaged goods. Which I know is probably silly. I try not to let it get to me. But if I get a delayed email from a recruiter, even just by a day, it can kind of feel like proof that my whole career is closing down. 

I don't let it stop me from working hard, though! I promise. I just... I care a lot about my future and providing for my family. That's a completely normal thing for a guy my age to be focused on, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing and not letting things slip.

Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. That's... that's probably just a normal reaction to how competitive the IT field is right now, though, right? I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely irrational.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your health or the health of loved ones?\n\nPatient: Health? Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Like, doing the exact same thing over and over? No, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, I have my daily routines to keep my schedule on track, but I don't feel forced to repeat things or anything like that. I try really hard to just do a task efficiently and move on, you know, like a good manager should. I know some people have to, like, check locks a dozen times or wash their hands over and over, but I promise I don't have any habits like that. I really try to keep my behaviors as normal as possible. \n\nThat's... that's a good sign, right? That I don't have anything like that going on? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any social situations that you avoid because they make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Social situations? Oh, um... no, no, I don't avoid them. \n\nI mean, as an IT manager, I'm in meetings all day, so I really can't just hide away, you know? I always make sure I show up and do my part. I might get just a little bit... well, just the normal amount of nervous before a big presentation to the executives or something, but I never skip them. I push right through because I want to be reliable for my team. \n\nAnd I don't shy away from office get-togethers or dinners or anything like that, either. I really try to be friendly and get along with everyone. I don't want anyone to think I'm antisocial or not a team player. \n\nThat's... that's a good thing, right? That I'm able to handle being around people without avoiding it? I just want to make sure I'm functioning the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or tasks that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But nothing too crazy, I promise. I try really hard to keep everything running smoothly. \n\nI mean, work-wise, I'm still getting things done, but I might have... delayed a few client deliverables lately. Just because I want them to be perfect, you know? I get a little stuck trying to anticipate every single thing that could possibly go wrong with the systems, so I end up taking a bit longer. And I guess I've missed a couple of networking follow-ups, too. It's just... I want to make sure I say exactly the right thing so I don't look incompetent. That's a pretty normal way for a manager to operate, right? Being thorough?\n\nAnd outside of work... well, I used to play in a weekend soccer league. I haven't been going as much recently. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I might get just a little bit... well, just the normal amount of nervous before a big presentation to the executives or something, but I never skip them. I push right through because I want to be reliable for my team. \n\nAnd I don't shy away from office get-togethers or dinners or anything like that, either. I really try to be friendly and get along with everyone. I don't want anyone to think I'm antisocial or not a team player. \n\nThat's... that's a good thing, right? That I'm able to handle being around people without avoiding it? I just want to make sure I'm functioning the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or tasks that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. That's... that's probably just a normal reaction to how competitive the IT field is right now, though, right? I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely irrational.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your health or the health of loved ones?\n\nPatient: Health? Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? Like, doing the exact same thing over and over? No, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, I have my daily routines to keep my schedule on track, but I don't feel forced to repeat things or anything like that. I try really hard to just do a task efficiently and move on, you know, like a good manager should. I know some people have to, like, check locks a dozen times or wash their hands over and over, but I promise I don't have any habits like that. Is it normal to avoid things when you're feeling this stressed out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: No, no headaches or tremors. I mean, I try to take good care of myself, drink enough water, all of that. So physically, I think I'm holding up okay. I don't get the shakes or anything extreme like that, I promise. It's really just the mental spinning, you know? The thoughts. But physically... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Is that a good sign? I'm hoping that means it's not too far gone, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain behaviors or mental acts to try and calm down when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I don't have any, like, weird rituals or anything like that. I'm pretty normal in that regard, I promise! I know some people have to, you know, wash their hands a certain number of times or check the locks over and over, but I definitely don't do any of that. \n\nI just... I mostly just try to think things through. Maybe I'll go over a conversation in my head a few times just to make sure I didn't say anything stupid, or double-check an email before I send it, but that's just being thorough, right? It's not like a compulsion or anything. I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job. That's a normal way to handle stress, isn't it? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life, not just work or finances?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it does bounce around a little bit? I try really hard to keep things in perspective, I really do. But... yeah, my mind kind of keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be focused on my contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing over the mortgage payments. And then... well, this is a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about my reputation, like whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm, you know, damaged goods. \n\nI know I shouldn't care so much about what other people think, and I'm really trying to be better about it. But it just spirals sometimes. Like, even just a delayed email from a recruiter... it can suddenly feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. That's... that's probably just a normal reaction to how competitive the IT field is right now, though, right? I just want to make sure I'm staying on top of my responsibilities and providing for my future. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely irrational.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your health or the health of loved ones?\n\nPatient: Health? Oh, um, no, not really. I mean, I try to stay on top of my checkups and everything, so I think I'm pretty healthy overall. I don't spend a lot of time obsessing over every little ache and pain, I promise. \n\nAnd as for my family... no, I don't think so. I mean, naturally, you want the people you care about to be okay, but I don't sit around dwelling on it. I just try to trust that everyone is doing their best to take care of themselves. That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? I just try to focus on the things I can actually control, like my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do feel a little tense sometimes. But just a normal amount, you know? Like anyone who's trying to stay on top of things. I mean, I do notice my shoulders get kind of locked up during the day, and I guess I feel a bit... wound up? Like I'm just waiting for the other shoe to drop, or for some bad news to come through. \n\nBut I try not to let it show! I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable. Sometimes I'll just, you know, pace around the house a little bit, or maybe bounce my leg while I'm on a conference call. Even when things are quiet and nothing is really happening. It's just a way to burn off a little extra energy, right? A lot of people do that when they're working hard. It doesn't mean I'm falling apart or anything. I'm still getting my work done. That's a pretty standard way to handle stress, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm managing it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. Like, doing the exact same thing over and over? No, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, I have my daily routines to keep my schedule on track, but I don't feel forced to repeat things or anything like that. I try really hard to just do a task efficiently and move on, you know, like a good manager should. I know some people have to, like, check locks a dozen times or wash their hands over and over, but I promise I don't have any habits like that. I really try to keep my behaviors as normal as possible. \n\nThat's... that's a good sign, right? That I don't have anything like that going on? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Are there any social situations that you avoid because they make you anxious?\n\nPatient: Social situations? Oh, um... no, no, I don't avoid them. \n\nI mean, as an IT manager, I'm in meetings all day, so I really can't just hide away, you know? I always make sure I show up and do my part. I might get just a little bit... well, just the normal amount of nervous before a big presentation to the executives or something, but I never skip them. I push right through because I want to be reliable for my team. \n\nAnd I don't shy away from office get-togethers or dinners or anything like that, either. I really try to be friendly and get along with everyone. I don't want anyone to think I'm antisocial or not a team player. \n\nThat's... that's a good thing, right? That I'm able to handle being around people without avoiding it? I just want to make sure I'm functioning the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or tasks that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But nothing too crazy, I promise. I try really hard to keep everything running smoothly. \n\nI mean, work-wise, I'm still getting things done, but I might have... delayed a few client deliverables lately. I don't want to snap at anyone or ruin their good time, so it just seems better to step back a little until I can get myself sorted out. \n\nI really am trying my best not to let it affect anyone else, though. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keeping it to myself so I'm not a burden?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no. No, I don't have anything like that. \n\nI mean, obviously I think about my job a lot, and I'll go over my checklists for the IT department in my head, but it's not like a broken record that I can't turn off. It's just... normal preparation, you know? I definitely don't have weird or bad thoughts getting stuck in my head against my will. I try really hard to keep my mind focused and productive, like a professional should. \n\nIf I need to focus on something else, I... I can. I'm in control of my thoughts, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm losing my grip or anything like that. That's... that's how it's supposed to be, right? Being able to manage what you're thinking about? I just want to make sure I'm being clear and giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain mental or physical actions in response to your worries?\n\nPatient: Actions? I'm not doing anything out of the ordinary, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or check things over and over when you're feeling anxious?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not an overwhelming urge, no.
